Let's analyze multiplicative update mathematically for minimizing t = max c_k. Use fixed point conditions for optimal a under sum=1, a≥0: For any active k where c_k=t, the gradient of c_k = 2 R_k a. However, there is a simpler coordinate method: To reduce a large coefficient c_k, scale down elements involved in that sum. The factor for each i is a_{k-i}. Multiplying all a_i by a_i/(1+η a_{k-i}) reduces c_k. If multiple active lags, combine. This is similar to "minimax download".

def fftconv(x,y):
    L=len(x)+len(y)-1
    m=1
    while m<L: m<<=1
    # Use cache? array of that m? not needed.
    fa=np.fft.rfft(x, m)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(y, m)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb, m)[:L]

    ar = a[::-1]
    conv=fftconv(ar? actually w, ar) return conv[n-1:2n-1]

def normalize_array(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<1e-12: a=np.full_like(a,1.0/a.size)
   else: a=a/s
   return a

def make_fourier(n, coefs, alpha=None):
   i=np.arange(n)+0.5
   d=np.zeros(n)
   for m,c in enumerate(coefs[1:], start=1): d += c*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*i/n)
   if alpha is None: alpha=1.0/(np.max(np.abs(d))+1e-12)
   a=1+alpha*d
   return normalize_array(a)
```

Candidate generator:
```
def get_parent_seq(limit=20000):
    try:
       p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
       if p.ndim==1 and 0<p.size<=limit and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
           return np.clip(p,0,1000)
    except Exception: pass
    return None

def upsample(a,n):
   m=len(a)
   if m==n: return a.copy()
   if m==0: return np.ones(n)
   idx=np.linspace(0,m-1,n)
   return np.interp(idx, np.arange(m), a)
```

def iterative_refine(a0, budget, rng, state, p_factors, lrs, betas, noise_scale, mode_phase=200):
    if budget<=0: return
    n=a0.size
    # maybe if n large, adjust check exact interval
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-15))
    loga=normalize_log(loga)
    best_fast=np.inf; best_local=None; it=0; last_exact=time.monotonic(); stagn=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline_start+budget and it < max_iter:
       a=exp_sum1(loga)
       c=fftconvolve_real(a,a)
       # fast score
       fs=2*n*c.max() # since sum1
       if fs < best_fast: best_fast=fs; best_local=a.copy(); stagn=0
       else: stagn +=1
       # exact periodically: if local best changed significantly or time since last exact > interval and (fs < state0)
       exact_interval = 3.0 if n>4000 else 0.5? maybe
       if (best_local is not None and time.monotonic()-last_exact > exact_interval and best_fast < state[0]):
           evaluate_candidate(best_local,state,force=False)
           last_exact=time.monotonic()
       # choose mode: phase= (it//mode_phase)%3
       phase = (it // mode_phase) % 3
       if phase==0: mode flat p_factor = p_factors[it % len(p_factors)]
       elif phase==1: mode peak beta = betas...
       else mixed
       compute grad
       maxg=...
       if maxg>0:
          lr = lrs[(it//some)%len(lrs)] maybe adaptive: if stagn>100 lr*=1.5 else decay
          loga -= (lr/maxg)*grad
          loga = normalize_log(loga, min_range=40)
       # stagnation perturbation
       if stagn > stagn_limit:
          loga += noise_scale * rng.normal(size=n)
          normalize_log; stagn=0
          # or reinit from best_local? maybe keep best
       it +=1
    # final exact evaluate best_local and current a
    if best_local is not None: evaluate_candidate(best_local,state,force=True)
    evaluate_candidate(exp_sum1(loga),state)
```

Now, implement `score_candidates` without lambdas:
```
def score_candidates(cands, state, max_top=6):
    scored=[]
    for a in cands:
       if a is None: continue
       a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
       if a.size==0: continue
       a=normalize(a)
       fs=fast_score_arr(a)
       if np.isfinite(fs): scored.append((fs,a))
    if not scored: return []
    scored.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    # evaluate exact for top with fast potentially better or top few if state inf
    for fs,a in scored[:min(max_top, len(scored))]:
       if state[0] == np.inf or fs < state[0]*(1+1e-5):
           evaluate_candidate(a, state)
    return scored[:min(max_top, len(scored))]
```
`evaluate_candidate`:
```
def evaluate_candidate(a, state):
    if a is None: return
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); a=normalize(a)
    if a.size==0: return
    # moment due exact can be slow; compute fast first to avoid exact if much worse
    fs=fast_score_arr(a)
    best_sc=state[0]
    if np.isfinite(fs) and (state[1] is None or fs < best_sc*(1+1e-4)):
       try:
          es=float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
       except Exception:
          es=np.inf
       if np.isfinite(es) and (state[1] is None or es < best_sc):
          state[0]=es; state[1]=a.copy()
    # If state still inf? maybe exact? if fs finite and state[1] is None? above did.
```

Let's draft iterative code carefully:
```
def iterative_refine(a0, sub_deadline, rng, state):
    start=time.monotonic()
    if sub_deadline - start < 5: return
    a0=normalize(a0)
    n=a0.size
    if n<8: return
    best_fast=fast_score_arr(a0)
    best_a=a0.copy()
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-15))
    it=0; stagn=0
    p_factors = [1.0, 1.05, 1.1, 1.15, 1.25]
    betas = [5.0, 10.0, 15.0, 25.0, 40.0]
    lrs = [0.05, 0.08, 0.12, 0.18, 0.25, 0.35]
    phase_len=100
    exact_gap = 8.0 if n > 4000 else 2.0
    last_eval = time.monotonic()
    # maybe if n large max iterations? no.
    while time.monotonic() < sub_deadline - 1.0:
        a, loga = exp_sum1(loga)
        c = convolve_fft(a,a)
        # correct slight neg
        c_max = float(c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(c_max) or c_max <=0: break
        fs = 2.0*n*c_max  # sum1
        if fs < best_fast:
            if fs < best_fast*0.999:
                best_fast = fs; best_a = a.copy(); stagn=0
            else:
                # marginal, maybe update every 10? 
                best_fast=fs; best_a=a.copy(); stagn=0
        else:
            stagn += 1
        # exact check
        if best_fast < state[0] and time.monotonic()-last_eval > exact_gap:
            evaluate_candidate(best_a, state)
            last_eval=time.monotonic()
        # choose mode based on it
        mode = (it // phase_len) % 2
        if mode == 0:
            pf = p_factors[(it//phase_len) % len(p_factors)]
            avg = 1.0/(2*n-1)
            e = c - pf*avg
            h = correlate_with_weights(a, e)
            grad = 4.0*h
        else:
            beta = betas[(it//phase_len) % len(betas)]
            x = c / c_max
            p = np.exp(beta*x - beta)
            ps = p.sum()
            if ps <=0: p = np.full_like(c, 1.0/c.size)
            else: p /= ps
            h = correlate_with_weights(a, p)
            grad = 2.0*h
        maxg = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if maxg > 0 and np.isfinite(maxg):
            idx=(it // max(1, phase_len//2)) % len(lrs)
            lr = lrs[idx]
            # increase lr if stagn high
            if stagn > 200: lr *= min(2.0, 1+stagn/500)
            loga = loga - (lr / maxg) * grad
        # noise/perturb stagnation
        if stagn > 1000:
            noise = 0.2 if n<=2000 else 0.15
            loga = loga + noise * rng.normal(size=n)
            # occasional reinitialize from best with noise
            if rng.random() < 0.3:
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(best_a,1e-15)) + 0.1 * rng.normal(size=n)
            stagn=0
        it += 1
    # final evaluate best and current
    evaluate_candidate(best_a, state)
    if it>0:
        a,_=exp_sum1(loga); evaluate_candidate(a,state)
```
Potential issue: `fs = 2n*cmax` assumes a sum1. exp_sum1 normalizes. Good. If due FFT cmax slightly less than exact, best_fast local. Fine.

`fftconvolve_real`: For input list? Convert `x=np.asarray(x,float)`. If x or y size 0? return zeros.

```
def fftconvolve_real(x,y):
    x=np.asarray(x,float); y=np.asarray(y,float)
    nx=x.size; ny=y.size
    if nx==0 or ny==0: return np.zeros(0)
    L=nx+ny-1
    m=1
    while m<L: m <<=1
    fx=np.fft.rfft(x,m); fy=np.fft.rfft(y,m)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fx*fy,m)
    return out[:L]
```
For large inputs, each call allocates arrays. OK.

`normalize`:
```
def normalize(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   if a.size==0: return np.zeros(0)
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <= 1e-12:
      return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size if a.size>0 else 0.0)
   a=a/s
   return a
```
If a all zeros, uniform. Good.

`exp_sum1`:
```
def exp_sum1(loga, max_range=50.0):
    loga=np.asarray(loga,float).ravel()
    n=loga.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0), np.zeros(0)
    loga=np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, -max_range)
    m=loga.max()
    loga=loga-m
    loga=np.clip(loga, -max_range, 0.0)
    a=np.exp(loga)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-300:
       a=np.full(n,1.0/n); loga=np.full(n,np.log(1.0/n)); return a, loga
    a=a/s
    loga=loga-np.log(s)
    return a, loga
```
After loga subtract log(s), max may not be0? Actually a sum1; loga might have max >0 if some >1? But normalized a_i can be >1/n, loga max maybe positive. exp_sum1 subtracts m each call, so range. It returns loga corresponding to normalized a? Let's check: After clip, a=exp(loga_clip), s; a_norm=a/s; loga_norm=loga_clip - log(s), which is log(a_norm). Its max may be <= -log(s) not necessarily 0. Next exp_sum1 subtracts max. Fine.

`fast_score_arr`:
```
def fast_score_arr(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   if a.size==0: return np.inf
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <= 1e-12: return np.inf
   c=fftconvolve_real(a,a)
   mb=float(np.max(c))
   if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb<0: mb=0.0
   return float(2.0*a.size*mb/(s*s))
```
This exact FFT score may be slightly different from exact if a has values >? no.

`correlate_with_weights`:
```
def correlate_with_weights(a,w):
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return zeros
   ar=a[::-1]
   conv=fftconvolve_real(w, ar)
   return conv[n-1:2*n-1]
```

`make_base_candidates` code details:
```
def make_base_candidates(n, rng, parent=None, max_count=40):
    cands=[]
    if n<=0: return cands
    x=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    xn = x/(n-1) if n>1 else np.zeros(1)
    # if parent: parent_interp
    if parent is not None and parent.size>1:
       p=upsample(parent,n)
       cands.append(p)
       # parent multiplied by mild anti-sym sine
       d=np.sin(2*np.pi*(x+0.5)/n)
       for amp in (0.1,0.2): cands.append(normalize(p*(1+amp*d)))
    # linear
    cands.append(normalize(np.linspace(0.2,1.0,n)))
    cands.append(normalize(np.linspace(1.0,0.2,n)))
    # random positive
    for _ in range(3): cands.append(normalize(rng.lognormal(0.0,0.8,size=n)))
    # polynomial 2nd
    for _ in range(6):
       u=float(rng.uniform(-2,2)); v=float(rng.uniform(-2,2))
       a=1.0 + u*xn + v*xn*xn
       a=np.clip(a,0,None)
       if a.sum()>0: cands.append(normalize(a))
    # random anti-symmetric
    for amp in (0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0):
       d=np.zeros(n)
       half=n//2
       r=rng.normal(size=half)
       d[:half]=r
       d[n-half:]= -r[::-1]
       if n%2==1: d[half]=0
       d-=d.mean()
       mx=np.max(np.abs(d))
       if mx>0:
          a=1.0+amp*(d/mx)
          if np.min(a)>=-1e-12:
             cands.append(normalize(np.maximum(a,0.0)))
    # fourier
    for M in (1,2,3,4,5,6,7):
       for _ in range(2):
          coefs=rng.normal(size=M)
          phases=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi,size=M)
          d=np.zeros(n)
          for m in range(1,M+1):
             d += coefs[m-1]*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*(x+0.5)/n + phases[m-1])
          d-=d.mean()
          mx=np.max(np.abs(d))
          if mx>0:
             d=d/mx
             for amp in (0.4,0.7,1.0):
                a=1.0+amp*d
                if np.min(a)>=-1e-12:
                   cands.append(normalize(np.maximum(a,0.0)))
    # trim if >max_count: maybe random sample first? To preserve diversity, if too many, keep first max_count? We can truncate after mixing. Use if len>max_count: take evenly? 
    if len(cands)>max_count:
       # keep first, last? Simpler random sample with rng but deterministic? Use index selection.
       idx = np.unique(np.linspace(0,len(cands)-1,max_count).astype(int))
       cands=[cands[i] for i in idx] # list comprehension lambda? not allowed? List comprehension is okay not lambda? The rule says no lambda functions; comprehension okay.
    return cands
```
List comprehension uses no lambda. Good.
Potential issue: For n large, fourier loops M=7 and sin arrays each candidate; 40 candidates maybe okay. Score candidates does fast FFT, more expensive.

`upsample`:
```
def upsample(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   if a.size==n: return a.copy()
   if a.size==0 or n==0: return np.ones(n)
   if a.size==1: return np.full(n,a[0])
   idx=np.linspace(0,a.size-1,n)
   return np.interp(idx, np.arange(a.size,dtype=float), a)
```

def search_length(n, sub_deadline, rng, state, parent):
    now=time.monotonic()
    sub_deadline = min(sub_deadline, deadline? not passed global). We'll pass sub_deadline.
    if n <= 32 or sub_deadline-now < 20: return
    max_cands = int(max(10, min(60, 250000 // n)))
    cands=make_base_candidates(n,rng,parent,max_cands)
    top=score_candidates(cands,state,max_top=min(5,max(1,max_cands//10)))
    if not top: return
    # If top candidate exact already very good <1.1, maybe just polish.
    # Time split: first 45%, second 30%, third 15%, rest 10.
    avail = sub_deadline - now
    splits=[0.45,0.30,0.15,0.10]
    k=min(len(top),4)
    for i in range(k):
       if time.monotonic() >= sub_deadline - 5: break
       # if i==k-1 use remaining
       if i < k-1:
          alloc = avail * splits[i]
       else:
          alloc = sub_deadline - time.monotonic() - 2
       if alloc <= 8: continue
       a=top[i][1]
       # If current global best length == n and fast score lower than top candidate, maybe use global best too?
       # Run iterative
       iterative_refine(a, time.monotonic()+alloc, rng, state)
```

```python
"""
Hybrid search for low self-convolution peak sequences.

The evaluator is scale invariant, so all candidates are normalized to sum 1.
The search combines:
  - deterministic asymmetric starts (ramps, powers, Fourier/anti-symmetric
    perturbations, parent interpolation);
  - log-space subgradient descent for a flat L2 self-convolution objective;
  - log-space subgradient descent for a smooth maximum-peak objective;
  - randomized local block refinements;
  - exact evaluation via the provided evaluate_sequence() for best tracking.
"""

import math
import operator
import time

import numpy as np


def normalize_sequence(a):
    """Make a 1D float array nonnegative, finite, and sum to 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a /= s
    return a


def exp_sum1(loga, max_range=50.0):
    """Convert log-space weights to a nonnegative array with sum 1."""
    loga = np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = loga.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64), np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, -max_range)
    m = float(loga.max())
    loga = loga - m
    loga = np.clip(loga, -max_range, 0.0)
    a = np.exp(loga)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
        loga = np.full(n, math.log(1.0 / n), dtype=np.float64)
        return a, loga
    a = a / s
    loga = loga - math.log(s)
    return a, loga


def fftconvolve_real(x, y):
    """Real linear convolution via zero-padded real FFT."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    nx = x.size
    ny = y.size
    if nx == 0 or ny == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    lout = nx + ny - 1
    m = 1
    while m < lout:
        m <<= 1
    fx = np.fft.rfft(x, m)
    fy = np.fft.rfft(y, m)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fx * fy, m)
    return out[:lout]


def convolve_auto(x, y):
    """Convolution using direct for very small lengths and FFT otherwise."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size == 0 or y.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size <= 160 and y.size <= 160:
        return np.convolve(x, y)
    return fftconvolve_real(x, y)


def correlate_with_weights(a, w):
    """
    Return h_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j} for i=0..n-1.
    w is assumed to have length 2n-1, matching a*a coefficients.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    ar = a[::-1]
    conv = fftconvolve_real(w, ar)
    return conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1]


def fast_score_arr(a):
    """FFT approximation of the evaluator score. No exact clipping."""
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = convolve_auto(a, a)
    mb = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mb / (s * s))


def exact_score_list(seq):
    """Call the authoritative evaluator safely."""
    try:
        v = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    try:
        v = float(v)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(v):
        return np.inf
    return v


def update_state_from_array(a, state, relative_slack=1e-3):
    """
    Update state = [best_score, best_array] if a is plausibly better.
    Uses fast score as a prefilter to avoid unnecessary exact calls.
    """
    if a is None:
        return
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return

    best_score = state[0]
    if state[1] is not None and np.isfinite(best_score):
        fs = fast_score_arr(a)
        if not np.isfinite(fs) or fs > best_score * (1.0 + relative_slack):
            return

    es = exact_score_list(a.tolist())
    if np.isfinite(es) and (state[1] is None or es < best_score):
        state[0] = es
        state[1] = a.copy()


def get_parent_sequence(limit=12000):
    """Fetch the provided global construction, if usable."""
    try:
        raw = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if raw.size == 0 or raw.size > limit:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(raw)):
        return None
    return normalize_sequence(np.clip(raw, 0.0, 1000.0))


def upsample_sequence(a, n):
    """Linearly interpolate a sequence to length n."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    if a.size == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    idx = np.linspace(0.0, float(a.size - 1), n)
    return np.interp(idx, np.arange(a.size, dtype=np.float64), a)


def make_base_candidates(n, rng, parent=None, max_count=50):
    """Generate diverse asymmetric, nonnegative candidate sequences."""
    cands = []
    if n <= 0:
        return cands

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    xn = x / float(n - 1) if n > 1 else np.zeros(1, dtype=np.float64)

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 1:
        p = upsample_sequence(parent, n)
        cands.append(p)
        d = np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * (x + 0.5) / float(n))
        for amp in (0.1, 0.2, 0.35):
            q = p * (1.0 + amp * d)
            if q.sum() > 0:
                cands.append(normalize_sequence(q))

    # Simple monotone ramps.
    cands.append(normalize_sequence(np.linspace(0.15, 1.0, n)))
    cands.append(normalize_sequence(np.linspace(1.0, 0.15, n)))

    # Positive random shapes.
    for _ in range(4):
        cands.append(normalize_sequence(rng.lognormal(0.0, 0.8, size=n)))

    # Low-degree asymmetric polynomials.
    for _ in range(8):
        u = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0))
        v = float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0))
        a = 1.0 + u * xn + v * xn * xn
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
        if a.sum() > 0.0:
            cands.append(normalize_sequence(a))

    # Power products, often useful continuous-bump approximations.
    for p_w in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        for q_w in (0.0, 1.0):
            left = (xn + 1e-6) ** p_w
            right = ((1.0 - xn) + 1e-6) ** q_w
            a = left * right
            if a.sum() > 0.0:
                cands.append(normalize_sequence(a))

    # Exact anti-palindromic random perturbations around all-ones.
    for amp in (0.35, 0.55, 0.75, 0.9, 1.0):
        d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        half = n // 2
        if half > 0:
            r = rng.normal(size=half)
            idx = np.arange(half)
            d[idx] = r
            d[n - 1 - idx] = -r[::-1]
        d = d / (np.max(np.abs(d)) + 1e-15)
        a = 1.0 + amp * d
        if float(a.min()) >= -1e-12:
            cands.append(normalize_sequence(np.maximum(a, 0.0)))

    # Low-order Fourier/phase shapes.
    for mcount in (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7):
        for _ in range(2):
            coefs = rng.normal(size=mcount)
            phases = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi, size=mcount)
            d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            for m in range(1, mcount + 1):
                d += coefs[m - 1] * np.sin(
                    (2.0 * math.pi * m * (x + 0.5) / float(n)) + phases[m - 1]
                )
            d = d - float(d.mean())
            mx = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
            if mx <= 0.0:
                continue
            d = d / mx
            for amp in (0.4, 0.7, 1.0):
                a = 1.0 + amp * d
                if float(a.min()) >= -1e-12:
                    cands.append(normalize_sequence(np.maximum(a, 0.0)))

    if len(cands) > max_count:
        idx = np.unique(np.linspace(0, len(cands) - 1, max_count).astype(int))
        cands = [cands[i] for i in idx]

    return cands


def score_candidates(candidates, state, max_top=5):
    """Score candidates by fast score, evaluate top few exactly, return top list."""
    scored = []
    for a in candidates:
        a = normalize_sequence(a)
        if a.size == 0:
            continue
        fs = fast_score_arr(a)
        if np.isfinite(fs):
            scored.append((fs, a))
    if not scored:
        return []

    scored.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
    top_n = min(max_top, len(scored))

    for fs, a in scored[:top_n]:
        if state[1] is None or fs < state[0] * (1.0 + 1e-4):
            update_state_from_array(a, state)

    return scored[:top_n]


def iterative_refine(a0, sub_deadline, rng, state):
    """
    Log-space descent alternating:
      - L2 flattening of the convolution around a target average;
      - smooth max-peak suppression.
    """
    a0 = normalize_sequence(a0)
    n = a0.size
    if n < 16 or sub_deadline - time.monotonic() < 8.0:
        return

    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a0, 1e-15))
    a, loga = exp_sum1(loga)

    best_fast = fast_score_arr(a)
    best_local = a.copy()

    avg = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)
    p_factors = (1.0, 1.04, 1.08, 1.12, 1.18, 1.28)
    betas = (6.0, 10.0, 14.0, 22.0, 35.0)
    learning_rates = (0.08, 0.12, 0.18, 0.26, 0.38, 0.55)

    phase_len = 90
    iter_count = 0
    stagnation = 0
    exact_gap = 6.0 if n > 4000 else 1.2
    last_exact = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < sub_deadline - 1.0:
        a, loga = exp_sum1(loga)
        c = convolve_auto(a, a)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        maxc = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(maxc) or maxc <= 0.0:
            break

        fs = 2.0 * n * maxc
        if np.isfinite(fs) and fs < best_fast - 1e-12:
            best_fast = fs
            best_local = a.copy()
            stagnation = 0
        else:
            stagnation += 1

        if best_fast < state[0] and time.monotonic() - last_exact > exact_gap:
            update_state_from_array(best_local, state)
            last_exact = time.monotonic()

        phase = (iter_count // phase_len) % 2
        subph = (iter_count // phase_len) % len(p_factors)
        if phase == 0:
            pf = p_factors[subph]
            e = c - pf * avg
            h = correlate_with_weights(a, e)
            grad = 4.0 * h
        else:
            beta = betas[(iter_count // phase_len) % len(betas)]
            xrel = c / maxc
            p = np.exp(beta * xrel - beta)
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(ps) or ps <= 0.0:
                p = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / c.size)
            else:
                p /= ps
            h = correlate_with_weights(a, p)
            grad = 2.0 * h

        grad = np.asarray(grad, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        maxg = float(np.max(np.abs(grad))) if grad.size else 0.0
        if np.isfinite(maxg) and maxg > 1e-30:
            lr = learning_rates[(iter_count // max(1, phase_len // 2)) % len(learning_rates)]
            if stagnation > 800:
                lr *= min(2.0, 1.0 + 0.2 * (stagnation // 800))
            loga = loga - (lr / maxg) * grad

        if stagnation > 1200:
            noise = 0.06 if n > 4000 else 0.09
            loga = loga + noise * rng.normal(size=n)
            if stagnation > 4000:
                loga = np.log(np.maximum(best_local, 1e-15)) + 0.12 * rng.normal(size=n)
            stagnation = 0

        iter_count += 1

    update_state_from_array(best_local, state)
    if iter_count > 0:
        a, _ = exp_sum1(loga)
        update_state_from_array(a, state)


def search_length(n, sub_deadline, rng, state, parent):
    """Generate candidates of length n, then refine the best ones."""
    if n <= 32 or sub_deadline - time.monotonic() < 20.0:
        return

    max_candidates = int(max(12, min(70, 260000 // max(1, n))))
    candidates = make_base_candidates(n, rng, parent, max_candidates)
    top = score_candidates(candidates, state, max_top=min(5, max(1, max_candidates // 12)))
    if not top:
        return

    # If the global best is already this length, include it as a top candidate.
    if state[1] is not None and state[1].size == n:
        gb = state[1].copy()
        gf = fast_score_arr(gb)
        if np.isfinite(gf) and (not top or gf < top[-1][0]):
            top.append((gf, gb))
            top.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))
            top = top[:5]

    k = min(len(top), 4)
    if k == 1:
        splits = (0.9,)
    elif k == 2:
        splits = (0.6, 0.35)
    elif k == 3:
        splits = (0.48, 0.32, 0.18)
    else:
        splits = (0.45, 0.30, 0.15, 0.10)

    for i in range(k):
        if time.monotonic() >= sub_deadline - 5.0:
            break
        if i + 1 < k:
            alloc = (sub_deadline - time.monotonic()) * splits[i]
        else:
            alloc = sub_deadline - time.monotonic() - 2.0
        if alloc <= 10.0:
            continue
        iterative_refine(top[i][1], time.monotonic() + alloc, rng, state)


def block_polish(state, deadline, rng):
    """Random multiplicative block moves around the current global best."""
    if state[1] is None or deadline - time.monotonic() < 12.0:
        return

    best = normalize_sequence(state[1].copy())
    best_score = state[0]
    best_fast = fast_score_arr(best)
    n = best.size
    if n <= 0 or not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return

    min_width = 1
    max_width = int(max(2, min(max(2, n // 5), 4096)))

    no_fast_improve = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        cand = best.copy()
        nb = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        for _ in range(nb):
            w = int(rng.integers(min_width, max_width + 1))
            if w <= 0 or w > n:
                continue
            st = int(rng.integers(0, n - w + 1))
            sigma = 0.18
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
            if not np.isfinite(factor) or factor <= 0.0:
                factor = 1.0
            cand[st:st + w] *= factor

        cand = normalize_sequence(cand)
        fs = fast_score_arr(cand)
        if np.isfinite(fs) and fs < best_fast * (1.0 - 1e-7):
            es = exact_score_list(cand.tolist())
            if np.isfinite(es) and es < best_score:
                best = cand
                best_score = es
                best_fast = fs
                no_fast_improve = 0
                if es < state[0]:
                    state[0] = es
                    state[1] = best.copy()
        else:
            no_fast_improve += 1

        if no_fast_improve > 2500:
            break


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.

    Uses the full provided budget, keeps the best exact-evaluated sequence,
    and always returns a valid nonnegative list.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 6.0)

    state = [np.inf, None]

    parent = get_parent_sequence(limit=12000)
    if parent is not None:
        update_state_from_array(parent, state)
    print(f"start best={state[0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    lengths = []
    if parent is not None and 256 <= parent.size <= 12000:
        lengths.append(int(parent.size))
    for L in (512, 1024, 2048):
        if L not in lengths:
            lengths.append(L)

    lengths = sorted(set(int(L) for L in lengths if int(L) >= 256))
    if len(lengths) > 3:
        keep = []
        preferred = (1024, (parent.size if parent is not None else 2048), 2048)
        for L in preferred:
            if int(L) in lengths and int(L) not in keep:
                keep.append(int(L))
        if len(keep) == 0:
            keep = lengths[-3:]
        lengths = sorted(keep)

    if not lengths:
        lengths = [1024]

    if len(lengths) == 1:
        weights = (1.0,)
    elif len(lengths) == 2:
        weights = (0.4, 0.6)
    else:
        weights = (0.25, 0.35, 0.40)

    for idx, L in enumerate(lengths):
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 30.0:
            break
        frac = weights[idx] / sum(weights[idx:])
        budget = max(20.0, rem * frac)
        sub_deadline = min(deadline - 2.0, time.monotonic() + budget)
        print(
            f"searching length={L} budget={budget - (time.monotonic() - time.monotonic()):.1f}s "
            f"current_best={state[0]:.6f}",
            flush=True
        )
        search_length(L, sub_deadline, rng, state, parent)
        print(f"after length={L} best={state[0]:.6f}", flush=True)
        if state[0] <= 1.02:
            break

    block_polish(state, deadline, rng)

    if state[1] is None:
        fallback = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
        fallback = normalize_sequence(fallback)
        update_state_from_array(fallback, state)

    final = state[1] if state[1] is not None else np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    final = normalize_sequence(final)
    update_state_from_array(final, state)

    final = state[1] if state[1] is not None else final
    out = [float(v) for v in final]
    # Final safety check: ensure sum not too small and values nonnegative/finite.
    if not out or not all(np.isfinite(v) and v >= 0.0 for v in out) or sum(out) < 0.01:
        out = [1.0 / 1024.0] * 1024

    print(f"final best={state[0]:.6f} len={len(out)}", flush=True)
    return out
```
